# Funding and support programs for Flatbread Proof

Produced by an Oven DAG run on 23 August 2026.
Every figure needs rechecking before an application goes out.

## What this report is

Flatbread Proof needs three things: cash for maintainer time, model and compute credits for recall tests, and exposure among TypeScript and coding-agent developers. Proof stores agent reasoning as typed Markdown in Git and returns bounded digests for later recall. This report compares every funding, credit, fellowship, accelerator, hosting, and adoption program kept by the research run. It records award size, applicant type, status, deadline, terms, fit, and evidence. It also lists rejected programs so the search is not repeated. Scores help set order; they do not promise success. Legal entity, country, fiscal-host, academic, relocation, and equity facts remain unknown. The report does not claim Proof is a company, nonprofit, hosted service, database, task tracker, or proven research result at scale.

## How to read the scores

Opportunity is 0–100: cash 0–40, AI or compute credits 0–30, relevant developer exposure 0–20, and room for research 0–10. The four parts always add to the shown total.
Confidence estimates acceptance of a strong application: 80–100 VERY HIGH, 60–79 HIGH, 40–59 MODERATE, 20–39 LOW, and 0–19 VERY LOW. A closed call or unmet hard gate scores 0.
Expected value (EV) is `round(Opportunity × Confidence / 100)`. It is a triage score, not money and not a statistical forecast.

Tony should personally write the Transformative AI Fund research case, the Lightcone Commons application, and the FUTO Fellows application. They require real choices about safety framing, public-benefit scope, and relocation. An agent can prepare the listing forms, evidence pack, and first drafts.

### a16z speedrun

Funder — Andreessen Horowitz (a16z).

### EIC Pathfinder Challenges 2026 — DeepRAP

Funder — European Innovation Council, under Horizon Europe (European Commission).
Track — EIC Pathfinder Challenges 2026, challenge titled DeepRAP: Deep Reasoning, Abstraction & Planning towards trustworthy Cognitive AI Systems.
Support type — CASH, MENTORSHIP
Award — Grants of up to EUR 4 million, or more if duly justified. The Challenges page and the Pathfinder page both state this ceiling. The official Pathfinder page also lists booster grants of a fixed amount not exceeding EUR 50 000 for testing commercialisation potential, plus coaching, mentoring, and networking. The Challenges budget for 2026 is €96 million. The money pays for early-stage scientific and technological R&D (the Pathfinder page states TRL 1–4, up to proof of concept). The 20 May 2026 Pathfinder Open news item restates funded Pathfinder projects as grants of up to €3–4 million.
Strings attached — Standard Horizon Europe grant agreement. The Pathfinder page lists Business Acceleration Services after award. No equity is stated on the Pathfinder page. Reporting and consortium rules follow Horizon Europe. A 22-page Part B is required for Pathfinder Open in the 2026 work programme PDF; Challenges follow the same work programme. Travel or residency is not stated as a condition on the pages opened.
Applicant type required — A legal entity established in an EU Member State or a Horizon Europe Associated Country. Pathfinder Challenges accept a single applicant, a two-partner consortium, or a larger consortium. The official Pathfinder FAQ states single legal entities established in a Member State or Associated Country may apply to Challenges, but mid-caps and larger companies are not eligible as single applicants. Consortia of at least three independent legal entities in different countries remain the default Pathfinder Open rule; Challenges relax that. Natural persons can appear in some Pathfinder settings (work programme text). An unincorporated project with no legal entity cannot sign a Horizon grant. Forming an eligible entity means incorporating a company, non-profit, or research organisation in a Member State or Associated Country (the UK is associated to the whole of Horizon Europe including Pathfinder, per the official FAQ; Swiss entities have been eligible for Pathfinder since 10 November 2025, per the same FAQ). That incorporation, a PIC on the Funding & Tenders Portal, and a bank account in the entity’s name are the minimum. An individual acting alone, with no entity, cannot apply.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — 28 October 2026 for Pathfinder Challenges.
How to apply —

1. Confirm an eligible legal entity in a Member State or Associated Country, or form one and obtain a PIC.
2. Read the DeepRAP Challenge Guide PDF linked from the Challenges 2026 page (the guide itself was not successfully opened in this run; the page lists it).
3. Write the proposal against the Challenge’s science-towards-technology test: a long-term vision of a radically new technology, a concrete breakthrough, and a high-risk / high-gain method.
4. Submit on the EU Funding & Tenders Portal before 28 October 2026.
5. Evaluation results are expected on the Pathfinder Open cadence of about five months from the deadline (that timing is stated for Open; Challenges timing is not separately stated on the pages opened).
   Opportunity — 60; cash/credits/exposure/research = 40/0/10/10
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. The 2025 Pathfinder Challenges call received 667 proposals and awarded 30 projects (€118 million). That is about 4.5 percent. The 2026 Challenges budget is €96 million, so a similar bar is the honest expectation. The project has no known EU or associated-country entity, no academic affiliation, and no consortium. Forming a company does not by itself supply the scientific consortium or the “radically new technology” framing Pathfinder demands. A programme the project cannot apply to scores 0; if an eligible entity exists or is formed in time, the published Challenges hit rate still keeps this in the VERY LOW band. No DeepRAP-specific acceptance rate was published on the pages opened.
   Fit verdict — PLAUSIBLE. DeepRAP is the only 2026 Pathfinder Challenge whose stated aim (deep reasoning, abstraction, planning, trustworthy cognitive AI) matches Proof’s agent-memory research agenda, but only a legal entity in a Member State or Associated Country can apply, and the bar is a science-towards-technology breakthrough, not a shipping open-source tool.
   Pitch angle — Proof keeps an agent’s reasons beside the code as typed Markdown records under `.flatbread-proof/`. An Effort holds one thread of work. Issue, Finding, Decision, Constraint, Risk, Citation, and Blob records sit under it, linked by `derives_from`, `supersedes`, `invalidates`, and `cites`; Reads return a digest capped at 25 records, one relation hop, 50 edges, and 64 KiB. DeepRAP asks for trustworthy cognitive AI that can reason, abstract, and plan. The grant would pay for a controlled study of whether those typed edges change a later agent’s decisions, and whether the digest caps still hold as the graph grows. The deliverable is an open, citable evaluation: matched repositories with and without valid relations, scored across three model families, with provider-side token and cost logs, released as data and a paper the Pathfinder portfolio can cite.
   Evidence —

### NSF CISE Future CoRe — RI or SHF

Funder — United States National Science Foundation, Directorate for Computer and Information Science and Engineering.
Track — NSF 25-543, Computer and Information Science and Engineering: Future Computing Research (Future CoRe). The closest named programmes inside that solicitation are Robust Intelligence (RI) and Software and Hardware Foundations (SHF). Human-Centered Computing (HCC) and Information Integration and Informatics (III) are also in the same solicitation.
Support type — CASH
Award — Up to $1,000,000 for up to 4 years. The solicitation says typical projects are about $150,000 to $250,000 per year for 3 to 4 years, and projects are discouraged from exceeding $300,000 in any single year. The money pays for research and education that develops new knowledge in computing. No cost sharing is allowed.
Strings attached — Standard NSF reporting (annual project reports and a public project outcomes report). No equity. Intellectual property follows NSF grant terms. No residency is stated. A PI may be on a limited number of Future CoRe proposals (the solicitation enforces a per-person proposal limit; the exact count is in the solicitation text).
Applicant type required — Only (1) US-accredited institutions of higher education with a US campus, acting on behalf of faculty; (2) US non-profit, non-academic organisations associated with education or research; (3) federally recognized Tribal Nations. For-profit companies cannot submit. Individuals and unincorporated projects cannot submit. Forming a US company does not open this door. The only path is a faculty appointment, a staff role that can PI through a US university or qualifying non-profit, or a university that agrees to lead with the maintainer as senior personnel. An overseas campus of a US university is not enough without a US-campus justification.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — Next target date after 23 August 2026 is the second Thursday in September annually (the 2025 date printed in the solicitation is 11 September 2025; the next such Thursday in 2026 is 10 September 2026). The following target is the first Thursday in February annually (5 February 2026 is printed; the next is 4 February 2027). Submit by a target date if a panel in that cycle is wanted.
How to apply —

1. Secure a US university or qualifying non-profit as the submitting organisation, and a PI who can submit through it.
2. Contact a cognizant CISE programme officer for RI or SHF before writing.
3. Prepare a full NSF proposal in Research.gov or Grants.gov against NSF 25-543 and the PAPPG in force on the submission date.
4. Submit at any time; aim at 10 September 2026 for the next panel cycle.
   Opportunity — 58; cash/credits/exposure/research = 40/0/8/10
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. NSF did not publish an acceptance rate on the pages opened. The project has no known US academic affiliation. Without that affiliation the score is 0. With a willing US university PI, a solo 64-star tool still has to be recast as new knowledge in intelligence or software foundations, not as product maintenance. That is a steep recast. Paperwork for a full NSF research proposal is large; it is only worth the time if the university already wants the work.
   Fit verdict — WEAK. The science of bounded agent recall and typed memory graphs can sit in RI or SHF, but only a US university or qualifying non-profit can apply, and the project has no such home.
   Pitch angle — Proof is a typed relational graph for an agent’s durable reasons, stored as ordinary files in the user’s Git repository. The research question is what structure an agent’s memory should have, how to keep recall bounded as the graph grows, and whether stored reasoning changes a later agent’s behaviour. That is a Robust Intelligence and Software Foundations question, not a product pitch. The grant would pay for instrumented recall experiments across three model families, graph-size stress tests of the 25-record / 64 KiB digest, and a public dataset of agent traces. The deliverable is papers and open artefacts a CISE programme can point to as new knowledge about agent memory, not a hosted service.

Funder — European Innovation Council, under Horizon Europe.
Track — EIC Accelerator Open 2026 (any field of technology). Challenges exist as a parallel track with a 2026 budget of €220 million; Open has €414 million.

Small mid-caps (up to 499 employees) may apply for investment-only, not the grant. Consortia are not allowed. An unincorporated project cannot apply. Forming an eligible SME means incorporating a for-profit company that meets the EU SME definition (under 250 staff and either ≤ €50 million turnover or ≤ €43 million balance sheet — the last two figures appear on second-hand guides, not on the official Accelerator page opened here; treat the exact SME thresholds as UNVERIFIED against the official page). The official page requires a start-up or SME (including spin-outs) in a Member State or Associated Country. Third-country applicants may relocate the company before Step 2.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — Next full-proposal batching dates still ahead: 2 September 2026 and 4 November 2026, 17:00 Brussels time. Short proposals have no closing date.

1. Incorporate (or relocate) a for-profit SME in a Member State or Associated Country.
2. Submit a short proposal at any time on the Funding & Tenders Portal: 12-page form, 10-slide deck, 3-minute video of up to three team members.
3. If the short proposal receives a GO (about 4–6 weeks), prepare the 20-page full proposal, implementation plan, financials, letters of intent, FTO analysis, and video, and hit a 2026 batching date.

1. Read https://funds.effectivealtruism.org/funds/transformative-ai.
2. Apply at https://funds.effectivealtruism.org/apply-for-funding (the page says apply in less than one hour).

   Opportunity — 48; cash/credits/exposure/research = 34/0/6/8
   Confidence — 12 VERY LOW (corrected). No acceptance rate is published, and agent-memory work fits only as a safety-relevant open evaluation, not ordinary product work.
   Fit verdict — PLAUSIBLE. Tony can apply as an individual. The pitch must say how bounded, inspectable agent memory reduces a stated risk from advanced AI, or why the evaluation itself is the contribution.
   Pitch angle — Flatbread Proof stores durable coding-agent reasoning as a typed graph of Markdown files inside the user's Git repository; A later agent reads a bounded digest rather than an unbounded transcript. Relations state which evidence supports a decision, which record supersedes an older one, and which claim is invalid; This creates a testable safety question: when an agent inherits project memory, does explicit invalidation and bounded recall reduce action based on stale or contradicted evidence. We seek support for an early empirical study, not a claim that the method is already proven; We will build matched repositories with the same prose in two forms: one with valid typed relations and one with relation fields removed. Agents from several model families will answer status and rationale questions, identify invalidated evidence, and choose an action under a stale constraint; Blinded scoring will measure correct source use, stale-evidence errors, tool calls, tokens, latency, and cost; The current evidence is a 12-run pilot on one recall question. It found median tool calls of 21 in the unbounded workflow and 10 with the bounded workflow, while all answers scored 5/5; That result sets the design, not the conclusion. We will publish the harness, fixtures, preregistered scoring rules, raw outputs, analysis, and a plain-language report; A negative result will be published on the same terms.

### Open Source for Science Fund — OS4LS

Funder — Open Source for Science Fund, a multi-donor initiative by Renaissance Philanthropy. Seeded by Biohub and Wellcome, with support from the Kavli Foundation and the Research Software Alliance. Operational successor to CZI’s Essential Open Source Software for Science.
Track — Open Source for the Life Sciences (OS4LS), inaugural call. Track 1 Domain-specific Tools or Track 2 Foundational Libraries and Ecosystem Initiatives.
Support type — CASH, EXPOSURE
Award — Track 1 up to $250,000 over two years ($125,000 a year). Track 2 up to $1,000,000 over two years ($500,000 a year). Indirect costs may not exceed 10 percent of direct costs and sit inside those caps. Funds may pay salaries, community events, cloud and AI/ML infrastructure including API tokens, cross-project coordination, and travel that supports the work. The Fund also pays travel to its own convenings outside the grant budget.
Strings attached — Annual reports. Proposals are shared with current and future funding partners and are not treated as confidential except as in Renaissance Philanthropy’s privacy policy. Renaissance Philanthropy grant conditions apply. Institutional sign-off at full proposal. The Fund does not take unsolicited proposals outside posted calls.
Applicant type required — Domestic and foreign non-profit and for-profit organisations, public and private institutions, companies, and eligible federal agencies. Grants are not made to individuals. Independent projects must affiliate with an eligible organisation or name a fiscal sponsor (examples given: NumFOCUS, Code for Science & Society) by the full-proposal deadline. Principal investigator must be a core maintainer or designated representative. Software must be OSI-open and public. Work must comply with US OFAC, export, and FCPA rules.
Status — CLOSED
Deadline — PASSED: letters of intent 8 June 2026; full proposals 21 July 2026. LOI submissions are closed. Notifications expected October 2026. The Fund says it will announce later calls, including fields beyond the life sciences, on https://os4science.org/apply-for-funding/.
How to apply —

1. Subscribe at https://os4science.org/newsletter/ and watch https://os4science.org/apply-for-funding/.
2. When a call opens, file an LOI, then a full proposal if invited.
3. Questions: info@os4science.org.
4. If a future call allows independent projects, secure a fiscal sponsor before the full-proposal deadline.
   Opportunity — 48; cash/credits/exposure/research = 32/8/4/4
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. The inaugural call required demonstrated adoption in the life sciences. Proof is a TypeScript agent-memory tool with 64 stars and no life-science user base. Early-stage prototypes without broad scientific adoption are named as out of scope. A later non-life-sciences call could change this. Grants still require an organisation or fiscal host.
   Fit verdict — INELIGIBLE — for OS4LS. Watch the Fund for a later call that covers research-software infrastructure outside biology. Even then, get a fiscal host first.
   Pitch angle — Do not pitch Proof as life-science software. If a later call covers agentic-workflow infrastructure for science in general, the honest pitch is a typed, bounded memory layer that scientific coding agents can write and humans can review in Git. The deliverable would be maintainer time plus evaluation of recall quality, not a biology application.
   Evidence —
   https://os4science.org/funding_opportunity/os4ls/
   https://os4science.org/apply-for-funding/
   https://os4science.org/faq/
   https://os4science.org/about/the-funds-history/

### Shuttleworth Foundation Fellowship

Funder — Shuttleworth Foundation Trust.
Track — Shuttleworth Foundation Fellowship Programme (one year, renewable on reapplication; intakes 1 March and 1 September).
Support type — CASH, MENTORSHIP
Award — A year’s salary equivalent, a contribution to expenses, and a travel allowance. The salary grant is set from the fellow’s qualifications, experience, and comparable cost of time. On top of that, the current model page sets aside $245,000 per fellow per year as potential project funding. The Foundation matches the fellow’s own project investment by at least ten times from that pool. A 2020 official FAQ stated project funding of $275,000; the live model page now says $245,000. Use $245,000.
Strings attached — 100% of time and attention on the fellowship focus. Outside work, paid or volunteer, needs Foundation approval. Must start on 1 March or 1 September. Must attend bi-annual Fellows gatherings. Must communicate openly. Fellows work from where they are; Cape Town office is optional. A 23 October 2020 official FAQ said the Foundation expects a 30% equity share in a commercial for-profit venture because it put in the initial funding, and that this does not rise in years two and three. The 2026 model page does not repeat that equity clause. Treat 30% as a live risk until the Foundation confirms it in writing. That equity, if still in force, is a hard conflict with later non-dilutive grants and with keeping the project independent. Open licensing is required.
Applicant type required — Individuals of majority age, fluent in English, anywhere in the world. The Foundation funds the person, not the organisation. People inside an organisation may still apply as individuals.
Status — CLOSED (corrected)
Deadline — PASSED: exact final application date UNKNOWN; foundation closure was announced for early 2024 (corrected)
How to apply —

1. Read the closure notice at https://www.shuttleworthfoundation.org/thinking/2022/07/19/thinking-closing/.
2. Do not submit through the legacy fellowship form.
3. No current application URL exists on the evidence opened for this run.
   Opportunity — 46; cash/credits/exposure/research = 34/0/6/6
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW (corrected). An official closure notice says the then-current fellows were the last; legacy application pages conflict and do not support a live 2026 call.
   Fit verdict — PLAUSIBLE — if the Foundation confirms it will not take 30% of a later company and if the pitch is openness and access to knowledge, not a startup. INELIGIBLE in spirit if the 2020 30% equity rule still stands and the maintainer will not give it.
   Pitch angle — Shuttleworth funds people who change access to knowledge through openness. Proof makes an agent’s reasons reviewable in the same Git repo as the code, under MIT, with no hosted lock-in. The salary grant buys a year of maintainer time. The $245,000 pool, if the fellow invests some of their own grant into the work, pays the recall evals and the public write-up. The deliverable is an open tool and a year of public notes, not a closed product.
   Evidence —
   https://www.shuttleworthfoundation.org/funding/fellowship-programme/
   https://www.shuttleworthfoundation.org/fellowship/fellowship-model/
   https://www.shuttleworthfoundation.org/fellowship/apply/
   https://www.shuttleworthfoundation.org/funding/fellowship-programme/who-can-apply/

### NSF America’s Seed Fund SBIR/STTR Phase I

Funder — United States National Science Foundation, Directorate for Technology, Innovation and Partnerships. Also known as America’s Seed Fund powered by NSF.
Track — NSF 26-510, Small Business Innovation Research / Small Business Technology Transfer Phase I, Phase II, Fast-Track: Developing Deep Technologies that Advance U.S. Competitiveness and Security. NSF 26-511 is a parallel scientific-instrumentation pilot and is the wrong topic (see Screened out).
Support type — CASH, MENTORSHIP
Award — Phase I up to $305,000 for 6–18 months, inclusive of direct costs, indirect costs, small-business fee, TABA, and optional I-Corps. Phase II up to $1,250,000 for about 24 months, only after a Phase I award. Fast-Track up to $1,555,555. The seedfund homepage states “up to $2 million” and “zero equity.” Anticipated about 180 SBIR Phase I and 50 STTR Phase I awards per year. The money pays for technical R&D, not routine engineering.
Strings attached — No equity; awardees keep company and IP, per the solicitation and seedfund homepage. Special SBIR/STTR terms, due diligence, and CHIPS and Science Act restrictions apply. Awards may include termination-for-convenience language. Annual SAM.gov renewal. STTR requires a subaward to a non-profit research institution. Phase I PI must spend at least one calendar month per six months of performance and be at least 51 percent employed by the small business.
Applicant type required — Only a firm that qualifies as a Small Business Concern under the SBIR/STTR Policy Directive and the Code of Federal Regulations. The solicitation points to the SBA “Guide to SBIR/STTR Program Eligibility” for the size and ownership tests. That guide was not successfully opened in this run, so the exact employee-count and ownership percentages beyond the solicitation’s own words are UNVERIFIED. The solicitation itself requires: a proposing small business; for STTR, a partner research institution; a PI whose primary employment (at least 51 percent) is with the small business at award and who has a legal right to work in the US. Individuals and unincorporated projects cannot submit a full proposal. Forming a qualifying US small business means incorporating a for-profit company in a US state, meeting the (UNVERIFIED in this run) SBA ownership and size tests, employing the PI at 51 percent, registering SAM.gov, Research.gov, and the SBA SBIR Company Registry, and holding a US bank account. SAM.gov can take three weeks or more.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — Next full-proposal deadlines: 4 November 2026; then 4 March 2027; 7 July 2027. The 27 July 2026 deadline is in the past as of 23 August 2026. Project Pitch invitations issued on or after 3 July 2025 that have not yet been used remain valid through 4 November 2026. Pitch review is typically 1–2 months. A company or individual may submit two Project Pitches per 12-month period and at most three pitches for the same project.
How to apply —

1. Incorporate a qualifying US small business and start SAM.gov, Research.gov, and SBA Company Registry registrations.
2. Submit one Project Pitch at https://seedfund.nsf.gov/apply/project-pitch/ covering technology innovation, technical objectives, market opportunity, and team (character limits 3500 / 3500 / 1750 / 1750).
3. Wait for an official invitation (about 1–2 months).
4. If invited, write the full proposal in Research.gov against NSF 26-510 (15-page project description per the 2026 how-to PDF).
5. Submit before 17:00 local time on 4 November 2026 or a later listed deadline, within the invitation window (next two deadlines after the invitation).
   Opportunity — 46; cash/credits/exposure/research = 34/0/6/6
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. The solicitation states about 180 SBIR Phase I awards per year. No official acceptance rate was on the NSF pages opened. Second-hand blogs claim 10–20 percent of invited full proposals; that figure is UNVERIFIED here. The Project Pitch is a hard filter: NSF must see high technical risk and commercial potential. Proof has no company, no revenue, and no hosted product. Forming a US small business is necessary and not sufficient. The commercial-potential criterion fights the project’s “not a hosted service” stance. A full SBIR proposal is many weeks of work; it is worth it only after a pitch invitation, not before.
   Fit verdict — PLAUSIBLE. Phase I is the largest US public cheque a newly formed small business can seek without equity, and agent-memory R&D can be the technical risk, but only a US Small Business Concern may apply, and NSF will score commercial potential that the project does not yet have.
   Pitch angle — Coding agents lose their reasons at the end of a session. Proof writes those reasons into the user’s repository as typed records and returns a bounded digest instead of the whole graph. America’s Seed Fund pays small businesses to prove a high-risk technical idea can become a product. Phase I would pay to instrument provider-side token cost, run the five recall experiments in the research agenda, and turn the result into a repeatable evaluation product a US team can sell or support. The deliverable is a Phase I final report plus a working evaluation harness and a go/no-go on a commercial offering, with the company keeping the IP.
   Evidence —
   https://www.nsf.gov/funding/opportunities/small-business-innovation-research-small-business-technology/nsf26-510/solicitation
   https://seedfund.nsf.gov/
   https://seedfund.nsf.gov/apply/full-proposal/
   https://seedfund.nsf.gov/apply/project-pitch/
   https://nsf-gov-resources.nsf.gov/files/NSF-how-to-submit-sbir-sttr-proposal.pdf

### NSF PESOSE

Funder — United States National Science Foundation, Directorate for Technology, Innovation and Partnerships, with other NSF directorates listed on the solicitation.
Track — NSF 26-506, Pathways to Enable Secure Open-Source Ecosystems. Three tracks: Track 1 scoping and planning; Track 2 establishing and expanding an open-source ecosystem; Track 3 improving safety, security, and privacy of an existing OSE. This replaces NSF 24-606 POSE, which no longer accepts applications.
Support type — CASH, MENTORSHIP
Award — Track 1: maximum $300,000 for up to 1 year, about 30 awards. Track 2: maximum $1,500,000 for up to 2 years, about 10 awards. Track 3: maximum $1,500,000 for up to 2 years, about 10 awards. Anticipated programme funding $40,000,000; 40 to 60 awards. Track 1 pays for planning and training in governance, licensing, fundraising, and administration, not product development. Track 2 pays to build governance, community, security, and long-term viability around an existing open-source product. Track 3 pays to find and fix vulnerabilities. No cost sharing.
Strings attached — Track 1 and Track 2 awardees must join mandatory “I-Corps for PESOSE” experiential activities. Standard NSF annual reports plus a public outcomes report. CHIPS and Science Act person-or-entity-of-concern rules apply. For-profit recipients use FAR Part 31 cost principles. Three to five letters of collaboration from current users or contributors who are not on the proposing team are required. SAM.gov UEI is required and can take weeks.
Applicant type required — A US organisation from this list: US-based for-profit commercial organisations including small businesses with research or education capability; US non-profit non-academic organisations tied to education or research; other federal agencies and FFRDCs (with PAPPG limits); state and local governments; US-accredited institutions of higher education with a US campus; federally recognized Tribal Nations. Individuals and unincorporated projects cannot submit. Non-profit and for-profit proposers must be US-based and US-owned and controlled: more than 50 percent of equity owned by US citizens, permanent residents, or firms that themselves meet that test, and actual control must sit in that block. The PI at a non-university organisation must be an employee normally resident in the US. University PIs must hold a tenure-track post, a primary full-time research or teaching appointment, or a staff leadership role in an Open-Source Program Office at a US campus. Forming a qualifying US company means incorporating in a US state, putting majority ownership and control in US citizens or permanent residents, employing a US-resident PI, registering a SAM.gov UEI, and opening a US bank account in the company’s name. That is weeks to months of work before a proposal can be filed.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — 1 September 2026, 17:00 local time of the submitting organisation. Then the first Tuesday in September annually, and 2 March 2027 then the first Tuesday in March annually.
How to apply —

1. Decide Track 1 (planning) unless three to five independent user or contributor letters and a managing-organisation plan already exist for Track 2.
2. Form or partner with an eligible US organisation and finish SAM.gov registration.
3. Collect three to five letters of collaboration from current users or contributors not on the team.
4. Write the proposal (7 pages for Track 1, 15 for Track 2 or 3). Title must start `PESOSE: Track N:`.
5. Submit through Research.gov or Grants.gov under NSF 26-506.
6. Budget I-Corps for PESOSE time for Track 1 or 2.
   Opportunity — 46; cash/credits/exposure/research = 34/0/8/4
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. NSF did not publish an applicant count or acceptance rate on the pages opened. About 30 Track 1 awards are expected from a national open-source call. The project is a 64-star solo-maintained tool with no named managing organisation, no formal governance file, and no known US entity. Track 2’s letters-from-current-users test is hard at that scale. Track 1 is the only honest first shot, and only after a US-owned organisation exists. A full NSF proposal plus I-Corps is a large time cost; it is worth it at $300,000, not as a speculative weekend application.
   Fit verdict — PLAUSIBLE. PESOSE is the US public programme written for open-source ecosystems around existing tools, including AI, but it requires a US-owned organisation and, for anything past Track 1, a real external user community the repository does not yet document.
   Pitch angle — Proof is an existing MIT-licensed product: typed project memory for coding agents, stored as Markdown in the user’s repository, written through 16 journalled mutations and read as bounded digests. PESOSE wants managing organisations that turn research-grade open-source products into safe, governed ecosystems. Track 1 money would pay the maintainer to write governance, contributor, security-disclosure, and sustainability plans, run the I-Corps for PESOSE discovery work, and recruit the external users who would later support a Track 2 proposal. The deliverable is a published OSE plan and a small, named contributor and user group, not a hosted service.
   Evidence —
   https://www.nsf.gov/funding/opportunities/pesose-pathways-enable-secure-open-source-ecosystems/nsf26-506/solicitation
   https://www.nsf.gov/funding/opportunities/pesose-pathways-enable-secure-open-source-ecosystems
   https://simpler.grants.gov/opportunity/5405aab0-e416-4386-bd4b-88f2f07dbb0a
   https://www.nsf.gov/funding/initiatives/pathways-enable-open-source-ecosystems

### Entrepreneur First US funding / $10k fellowship

Funder — Entrepreneurs First, with Transpose Platform on the second SAFE.
Track — US Funding ($250k pathway) and the equity-free $10k Fellowship Residency, both in San Francisco.
Support type — CASH, EXPOSURE, MENTORSHIP, EQUITY-INVESTMENT
Award — $250k pathway: $125k on a post-money SAFE for 8% from EF, plus an optional $125k uncapped MFN SAFE from EF and Transpose Platform if the founder relocates to San Francisco and incorporates as a Delaware C-corp. $10k pathway: equity-free $10k grant plus housing for about three months. Live and work in San Francisco. Demo Day for the funded path.
Strings attached — 8% on the first SAFE, plus an optional MFN SAFE. Three months in San Francisco. The $250k page says residency is not mandatory but strongly encouraged. Full-time. Programs on the apply portal are full-time and in person. No stated block on later grants. The 8% SAFE will change later grant conversations. The $10k grant is equity-free.
Applicant type required — Exceptional individuals, often before a cofounder, company, or idea. Sector agnostic. US applicants should use the US funding and fellowship path. People outside the US, Europe, or India may need a visa; EF does not provide visas but may help.
Status — ROLLING
Deadline — Rolling for US funding and fellowships. Separate dated programs on https://apply.joinef.com/ include The Bridge Residency in San Francisco, Fall 2026, apply by 30 August 2026 (for young Europeans).

1. Read https://www.joinef.com/faqs/us/.

   Pitch angle — Flatbread Proof is durable project memory for coding agents that stays with the code; It records issues, findings, decisions, constraints, risks, citations, and supporting blobs as Markdown in the user's repository. An Effort is one named thread of work; Typed links preserve why a decision was made and whether later evidence replaced or invalidated an earlier record. People review those changes through ordinary Git diffs and pull requests; This design gives the user custody of agent memory. There is no hosted memory service, opaque transcript archive, or proprietary database; A project can move between editors and model providers while keeping the same records; Reads are capped, so a later agent receives a small digest rather than every stored fact. During three months in Austin, I would turn the current package into a clear local-first workflow that a developer can install, inspect, and remove; The work would include a stable plugin package, migration and backup notes, a local open-weight example, and a public benchmark for later-session recall. The benchmark will compare typed relations with the same prose stripped of relations and will publish both successful and failed cases; FUTO would get a concrete demonstration of user-controlled AI tooling: the memory remains readable when a model subscription ends, and the full project history stays under the same version control as the code.

   Pitch angle — Flatbread Proof gives Cursor agents durable project memory without moving project reasoning into a hosted service; The plugin teaches the agent to create an Effort, meaning one named thread of work, and to record only durable issues, findings, decisions, constraints, risks, citations, and supporting blobs. Each record is Markdown under `.flatbread-proof/`; A person sees the same change in the pull request as any other repository edit. Typed relations preserve the reasoning between records; A decision can derive from a finding, a new record can supersede an older one, and a claim can be marked invalid. Later reads return bounded digests with hard limits instead of placing the whole history in the prompt; The plugin therefore adds a reviewable memory workflow, not a database, chat archive, or task tracker. The public repository is MIT licensed and written in TypeScript; The package already ships the agent skill and checks its release files for drift. For Marketplace review, we will provide the plugin manifest, the skill, a concise command reference, and a sample flow that starts in one Cursor session and recalls the current decision in another; We will also document removal: deleting the plugin stops the workflow, while the user's Markdown records remain ordinary files under the user's control.

Status — OPEN (corrected)
Deadline — 1 September 2026 (corrected)

2. Submit the measured agent-memory talk by 1 September 2026; the raw form URL is UNKNOWN on the page extract.

   Opportunity — 20; cash/credits/exposure/research = 0/0/16/4
   Confidence — 30 LOW (corrected). No acceptance rate. The Berlin deadline has already passed. The next open form is not posted.
   Fit verdict — STRONG — when a CFP is open. The published topic list names agentic development, context, and evaluation. Proof can speak to bounded recall without claiming a hosted memory product.
   Pitch angle — Proposed talk: **Can a coding agent remember why; A measured Git-based approach** Coding agents can resume code, but they often lose the reasons behind it; The next session searches chat, issues, and files, then may act on a stale decision. Flatbread Proof takes a small, inspectable approach: it stores issues, findings, decisions, constraints, risks, and citations as typed Markdown beside the code; An Effort is one named thread of work. People review the records in normal Git diffs; Later agents read a bounded graph digest rather than the full history; This talk will show the workflow and the first controlled result. In 12 runs across three model families, an unbounded recall flow used a median 21 tool calls; The bounded flow used 10; All answers in that small test scored 5/5, but the test covered only one question and one graph state. I will explain that limit, not hide it; The useful part for attendees is the method. I will show how to decide what deserves durable memory, how typed `supersedes` and `invalidates` links prevent old claims from looking current, and how hard read caps change agent behaviour; I will close with the next benchmark: harder rationale and risk tasks, larger graphs, provider token logs, and matched data with and without relations; Attendees will leave with an MIT-licensed package, a reproducible fixture, and a scoring sheet they can apply to their own agent workflow.

Foresight's value is the research network and review, not compute funding; I would use seminar feedback to sharpen the threat model and scoring rules, seek mentors who work on evaluations and secure multi-agent systems, and present both positive and negative results. The public outputs will be a benchmark harness, synthetic dataset, preregistration, result report, and fellowship seminar; The software and study artifacts will remain open so other agent builders can repeat the tests.

Agents can record issues, findings, decisions, constraints, risks, and citations, while relations preserve where a claim came from and whether it is current; Reads return bounded digests to keep later recall small; This proposal tests the write side. Proof's skill tells an agent to save a record only when it has future need, durable effect, causal value, and unique signal; That rule may improve future memory, or it may discard useful context; We will run four writing cases with and without the gate across three model families. A later agent will receive each resulting graph and answer questions about the current decision, its reason, stale evidence, and blocking risks; We will score recall, noise, wrong causal links, tool calls, tokens, and cost. The work is separate from the graph-size and provider-credit studies in our other drafts; Manifund support would pay for the writing experiment, later-reader runs, scoring review, and publication. We will post the proposal publicly, name other applications, and set a minimum that funds a complete smaller design; The deliverable will include prompts, synthetic repositories, raw outputs, scoring rules, analysis, and a short report; We will publish a null or negative result on the same schedule.

### Arcee AI Trinity Builders Program

Funder — Arcee AI.
Track — Trinity Builders Program.
Support type — AI-CREDITS
Award — Free Arcee API inference for Trinity models. Applicants request under 50 million, 50–200 million, 200–500 million, 500 million–1 billion, or above 1 billion tokens; actual allocation and dollar value are `UNKNOWN`.
Strings attached — Credits expire 90 days after allocation, are non-transferable, may be partial, and depend on capacity. Arcee may change or stop the program; sharing feedback is appreciated but not required. The page says program data is not retained or used for training.
Applicant type required — Individual developer or researcher, or an open-source team, doing real work with Trinity models; no entity or country requirement is stated.
Status — ROLLING
Deadline — No fixed deadline; best-effort rolling review.
How to apply —

1. Instrument the evaluation harness before applying.
2. Pick a token range and describe community value.
3. Submit through https://www.arcee.ai/trinity-builders-program.
4. Run the work inside 90 days if approved.
   Opportunity — 12; cash/credits/exposure/research = 0/0/4/8
   Confidence — 45 MODERATE. no acceptance rate is published, but the call explicitly names agentic workflows, evaluation, developer tooling and open-source builders, with no traction threshold.
   Fit verdict — STRONG. this directly funds an open-weight model family for the agent-recall matrix, though it covers only one family.
   Pitch angle — Flatbread Proof is durable project memory for coding agents; It stores project reasoning as typed Markdown in the user's Git repository and returns small graph digests to later sessions. An Effort is one named thread of work; Relations state which finding supports a decision, which new record supersedes an old one, and which evidence is invalid; The research question is whether that structure changes a later agent's answer and action. We request Trinity API access for a controlled, public evaluation; The first task set will cover status recall, decision rationale, supersession history, invalidated evidence, and risk synthesis; Each task will run against matched repositories: one with valid typed relations and one with the same prose but no relations. A second set will vary graph size and bounded-read paging; We will record API calls, input and output tokens, latency, task scores, and failure cases. The harness will be instrumented before credits start so the 90-day window is used for runs, not setup; We will use only Trinity-family credits for this work and report the exact model and settings. The output will include an OpenAI-compatible Trinity adapter, fixtures, prompts, scoring rules, raw results, and a short report; All code and data that we can publish safely will use a permissive licence; Results and practical model feedback will be shared with Arcee, whether they support or reject the memory design.
   Evidence —
   https://www.arcee.ai/trinity-builders-program
   https://www.arcee.ai/blog/introducing-the-trinity-builders-program

### NSF ACCESS Explore / Discover

Funder — US National Science Foundation, through the ACCESS allocations marketplace (RAMPS).
Track — Explore ACCESS for a first small allocation. Discover ACCESS is the next step (one-page description). Accelerate and Maximize exist for larger needs.
Support type — CLOUD-CREDITS
Award — UNKNOWN credit count. The policy says up to half the posted Credit limit is awarded first, the rest after a progress report. Explore needs only an abstract. Discover needs one page. Requests are reviewed continuously, often within two weeks; Explore decisions often come the next business day. Awards not tied to a merit-reviewed grant last 12 months and can be extended up to five years inside the credit limit. Work must be publishable; proprietary work is not supported.
Strings attached — Institutional email matching the stated affiliation. Progress and final reports. Acknowledge ACCESS and the resource provider. Credits must be exchanged for resource time before work starts (about a week for that approval).
Applicant type required — A US-based researcher or educator at graduate-student level or higher. Eligible homes: US academic institutions; US non-profit research or education organisations; FFRDCs in stated cases; US military academies; US commercial organisations (especially small businesses with R&D capability, a US federal or state research grant, or a university collaboration — the PI must still publish). Retired, self-employed, or otherwise unaffiliated people in the US are not eligible and may not use ACCESS resources on someone else’s project either. International researchers need a US-based PI with a real role. Forming a US company can open the commercial path; remaining an unaffiliated individual cannot. Contact the ACCESS Help Desk before a commercial submission.
Status — ROLLING
Deadline — Rolling. No calendar close.
How to apply —

1. Create an ACCESS account with an institutional email at https://allocations.access-ci.org/.
2. For Explore: title, abstract, CV (max 3 pages), optional grant info; tick ACCESS Credits.
3. Submit Explore ACCESS. If awarded, exchange Credits for a resource.
4. Upgrade to Discover or Accelerate once usage is known.
   Opportunity — 8; cash/credits/exposure/research = 0/0/2/6
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. Explore is designed to be easy and is often decided the next business day, so an eligible PI has a high chance of a small award. The project as an unaffiliated solo maintainer cannot apply (confidence 0). A US university appointment, even adjunct, or a US company that will publish, opens it. ### EuroHPC Industrial Playground / Fast Lane

Funder — EuroHPC Joint Undertaking.
Track — AI for Industrial Innovation — Playground Access, and Fast Lane Access. Large Scale Access (more than 50,000 GPU hours) is marked TEMPORARILY CLOSED on 23 August 2026 because of demand; Leonardo, MareNostrum, and LUMI-G are unavailable for new Large Scale allocations.
Support type — CLOUD-CREDITS
Award — Playground: small, fixed GPU-hour slices (exact hours are on the Playground page table; the fetched page did not include the numeric table). Duration 1, 2, or 3 months. Access within 2 working days. Fast Lane: up to 50,000 GPU hours, 1–3 months, approval within 4 working days. One system per request. SMEs and start-ups may commercially exploit results. A final report is due three months after the allocation ends. Results should be published except confidential parts, with a credit to EuroHPC JU and the AI Factory.
Strings attached — Civilian purpose only. AI Act compliance. English only. Acceptable Use Policy. PI employment contract must last at least 3 months after the allocation. VAT number and, if applicable, PIC. Double-awarding of running EuroHPC access is forbidden.
Applicant type required — An industry organisation — commercial company, SME, or start-up — established or located in a Member State or in a country associated to Horizon 2020 (for older machines) or to the Digital Europe Programme or Horizon Europe (for machines acquired after 2020). The PI must be affiliated with that industry organisation and employed there. Academia and the public sector are told to use the AI for Science mode instead. Individuals and unincorporated projects cannot apply. Forming an SME in a Member State or associated country, with a VAT number and a PIC, is the gate.
Status — OPEN
Deadline — Playground and Fast Lane: rolling, first come first served. Large Scale cut-offs resume on the published calendar if the closure lifts (next printed dates include 15 September 2026 and later).
How to apply —

1. Confirm an industry entity in an eligible country and a PI employment contract.
2. Register on https://access.eurohpc-ju.europa.eu/ (the Terms of Reference print the hyphenation as access.eurohpc ju.europa.eu).
3. Submit the short Playground or Fast Lane form; pick one system that still has capacity.
4. Pass eligibility and technical checks.
5. Sign the Acceptable Use Policy and use the allocation; file the final report.
   Opportunity — 6; cash/credits/exposure/research = 0/0/2/4
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. Playground has no competitive selection, so an eligible SME that can run on the named hardware has a decent chance of a small slice. The project has no known EU industry entity (confidence 0 until that changes). GPU hours do not replace API tokens for closed models. Paperwork is light for Playground; worth it only after an eligible company exists and the evaluation can run on the offered stack.
   Fit verdict — WEAK. Useful GPU time for an EU SME; closed to individuals; poor substitute for multi-model API evaluations.
   Pitch angle — If an eligible company exists: use Playground to time the harness on a European GPU, then Fast Lane for a larger run. The deliverable is an open report that credits EuroHPC. Offer the concrete release, evaluation, listing, or report described in this block.

### NRC IRAP

Funder — National Research Council of Canada, Industrial Research Assistance Program.
Track — Financial support for technology innovation (the named IRAP funding offer on the official page dated 8 January 2026).

Award — UNKNOWN on the official page. The page says IRAP provides advice, connections, and in some cases funding for R&D of technology-driven products and services. Funding, when offered, follows an advisor relationship and an invited proposal. IRAP does not fund day-to-day operations, non-technical commercial work, work outside Canada, or research with little commercial potential.
Strings attached — Relationship-driven. Advice first, funding later. Canadian economic benefit (jobs or innovation capacity) is required. No equity is stated. Reporting to the assigned Industrial Technology Advisor is implied by the relationship model; the page does not list a report schedule.
Applicant type required — An incorporated, for-profit business operating in Canada, with up to 500 full-time-equivalent employees, developing and commercialising innovative technology-driven products or services, ready to create economic benefits in Canada. IRAP does not support ULCs or LLCs, sole proprietorships, partnerships, or cooperatives. Individuals and unincorporated projects cannot apply. Forming a Canadian corporation (not an LLC/ULC), obtaining a CRA business number, financial statements, and a business plan are the minimum. Work must be done in Canada.
Status — ROLLING
Deadline — Rolling. Call 1-877-994-4727. No application window is published.
How to apply —

1. Incorporate a for-profit Canadian company (not an LLC/ULC) that meets the size and operating tests.
2. A senior executive calls 1-877-994-4727.
3. Complete the CEA conversation; if referred, work with an ITA.
4. If invited, submit the proposal the ITA requests. An invitation is not a promise of money.
   Opportunity — 6; cash/credits/exposure/research = 0/0/2/4
   Confidence — 0 VERY LOW. No award rate or amount is published on the official page. The project has no known Canadian corporation. Without one, confidence is 0. With one, IRAP still wants commercialisation in Canada and will not fund an unincorporated maintainer. Time cost is high relative to an unknown cheque: months of advisor meetings before any proposal.
   Fit verdict — WEAK. Real public R&D money for Canadian SMEs, useless until a Canadian corporation exists, and poorly matched to a no-revenue open-source tool with no Canadian operations on the record.
   Pitch angle — Only if a Canadian corporation exists. Then: Proof is the technical risk — bounded, typed agent memory that must be shown to cut wasted tool calls without dropping answer quality. IRAP would pay Canadian R&D salary to run that measurement and turn it into a product the company can sell. The deliverable is a Canadian-performed R&D report and a closer-to-market release.
   Evidence —

## Open questions before applying

1. **What is the exact applicant type and legal name, and in which country is it registered or resident?** This decides nearly every gate. An eligible EU or associated-country entity reaches EIC awards up to €4m and €2.5m. A qualifying US organisation reaches PESOSE up to $1.5m, SBIR Phase I up to $305k, Future CoRe up to $1m, and CSSI up to $600k. Do not form an entity only to chase these low-confidence calls.
2. **Which country can receive GitHub Sponsors payouts, and whose bank and tax account will receive cash?** This unblocks Sponsors plumbing, the $10k GitHub security fund if selected, and General Translation’s $1k cash award. It also decides whether those rows remain confidence 0.
3. **Will a university, research nonprofit, or fiscal host sponsor the work?** A host can reach Amazon’s roughly $70k cash plus $50k credits, Anthropic’s $20k science credits, NAIRR resources, OpenAI research credits, Sloan inquiries, and future Schmidt calls up to $5m or more. The host must agree to the work and terms; a name on paper is not enough.
4. **Is Tony willing to take equity or a SAFE and build a venture-backed company?** A yes opens a16z, YC, Village Global, South Park Commons, AI Grant, Neo, Betaworks, and EF. Their published checks reach $250k–$1m plus credits, but they change ownership and the roadmap. A no removes them from active work.
5. **Can Tony relocate or work on site, and for how long?** Austin opens FUTO Fellows up to $40k. Emeryville opens Astera salary of $125k–$250k plus a project budget. Berkeley, London, San Francisco, or New York affect MATS, Anthropic Fellows, accelerators, and hub grants.
6. **Will Flatbread accept a fiscal host and its fee?** A host can receive grants that reject individuals. Open Source Collective charges 10%. Use it only for a named award large enough to justify the fee and governance work.
7. **What are the dated npm downloads, direct dependents, named users, and external contributor counts?** This is the central selection fact for Sovereign Tech, Sequoia, FLOSS/fund, GitHub security funding, OpenAI Codex for OSS, and foundation homes. Those routes span $10k, living-cost support, credits up to $25k, and contracts above €50k.
8. **Will the project publish governance, a Code of Conduct, SECURITY.md, a disclosure contact, and an SBOM?** These files unblock or strengthen GitHub security funding, Vercel and Netlify OSS plans, GSoC, OpenJS, and public-infrastructure bids. They also reduce avoidable rejection risk.
9. **Which benchmark runs first, and is provider logging ready before credits start?** Pick one separate work package. Record provider calls, tokens, cache use, latency, cost, and task scores before accepting 90-day Arcee or six-month OpenRouter credits.
10. **Will Proof publish hosted docs, a real plugin, or a real MCP server?** Hosted docs unlock Vercel or Netlify and Algolia. A compliant plugin unlocks Cursor listings. An MCP server unlocks MCP registries, but the project should not build a fake server only for a listing.

## How this report was made

The Oven DAG used six discovery lanes after the Proof fit brief: OSS maintainer funds, science philanthropy, public non-dilutive funding, AI and compute credits, accelerators and fellowships, and exposure and adoption. A merger node deduplicated 122 lane headings into 112 calls and applied one scoring scale. A verification node re-opened 72 high-value or actionable calls. An adversarial audit then lowered weak confidence scores, found stale statuses, identified hard gates, and added six missed calls. A pitch node drafted the twelve highest-priority applications. This final node merged those results and kept the full screened-out trail.

Model used at each tier — the DAG that produced this report is
`.cursor/dags/flatbread/dag-funding-research.json`. It maps `HIGH` to GPT-5.6
Sol at high reasoning, `MED` to Cursor Grok 4.6 at high effort, and `LOW` to
Composer 2.5. The fit brief, the merger, the audit, the pitch drafts, and this
report ran at `HIGH`; the six discovery lanes and the verification node ran at
`MED`; the deadline calendar and the lane index ran at `LOW`. The run took
75 minutes over 8 ranks, and its closing oracle,
`.cursor/dags/flatbread/check-funding-report.sh`, checks that this file carries
every section, at least 40 evidence links, and at least 25 program blocks.

Lane files —

- `internal/funding/research/00-proof-fit-brief.md`
- `internal/funding/research/01-oss-maintainer-funds.md`
- `internal/funding/research/02-science-philanthropy.md`
- `internal/funding/research/03-public-nondilutive.md`
- `internal/funding/research/04-ai-compute-credits.md`
- `internal/funding/research/05-accelerators-fellowships.md`
- `internal/funding/research/06-exposure-adoption.md`
- `internal/funding/research/07-merged-scored.md`
- `internal/funding/research/08-verification.md`
- `internal/funding/research/09-adversarial-audit.md`
- `internal/funding/research/10-pitch-angles.md`

Known limits — The verification node checked 72 programs and skipped 40 lower-opportunity master rows. It could not confirm the GitHub Secure Open Source Fund form and Session 5 date, skills.sh install text, Cursor publish form, cursor.directory submit form, thanks.dev, Fastly, Prototype Fund pages, Betaworks, NVIDIA’s apply hub, OpenJS application file, JetBrains form, Manifund open-call page, NLnet Restack eligibility guide, OS4LS RFA, or the npm listing as a program. Where nodes conflicted, this report kept the GitHub Session 5 date UNKNOWN because no official date loaded; took the audit’s CLOSED verdict for Shuttleworth because its official closure notice outweighs legacy forms; kept MATS compute UNVERIFIED because current official pages disagree; and took the audit’s stricter a16z confidence because speedrun calls itself extremely selective. Several programs publish no per-applicant amount, acceptance rate, credit expiry, or exact payee rule. Those facts are marked UNKNOWN or UNVERIFIED. Every amount, date, status, applicant rule, and URL must be opened again on the day of application.

After the run, a reviewer requested all 408 unique URLs in this file. 383 answered. Most failures were sites refusing an automated request (403 from `openai.com`, `prototypefund.de`, `mercatus.org`, `npmjs.com` and `cloudflare.com`) or rate limits (429 from `cursor.directory` and `lesswrong.com`); those pages are live. Six were genuinely broken and produced these corrections:

- Together AI's `/startups/build` path returns 404. The live page is `https://www.together.ai/startup-accelerator`, and it shows credits tiered by money raised. Proof falls in the Build tier, so its ceiling is $15k rather than $50k. Opportunity dropped from 38 to 30 and EV from 10 to 8.
- Console.dev's `/submissions/` path returns 404, but `https://console.dev/selection-criteria` documents the real route: email `hello@console.dev`. That moved Console from confidence 0, blocked on a missing form, to 25.
- `https://github.com/secure-open-source` returns 404; `https://resources.github.com/github-secure-open-source-fund/` answers.
- `https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/founders` returns 404. The Evidence list already held the working `https://aws.amazon.com/startups/credits/`, so the dead line was dropped.
- The `os.nav.fund` host no longer resolves. The Navigation Fund row now points at `https://www.navigation.org/grants`.
- Every `ai.google.dev` documentation path answers with a redirect that the checker could not follow. Those pages are live and were left alone.
